        Idea:
# Sprint 2: Adapter Seam and Local Selector Skeleton

## Overall Context

This sprint creates the first worker-side VibeComfy seam. It depends on Sprint 1 profile behavior and deliberately proves local route selection before production selector/claim work arrives in Sprint 6.

## Shared Operating Rules

- Keep `reigh-worker/` external Supabase queue contracts unchanged.
- Backend-neutral resolved tasks must exist before WGP-specific queue conversion.
- Unsupported Comfy routes fail closed and remain WGP-only.
- Do not require Wan VACE cocktail or full orchestrated parity here.

## Sprint Goal

Add the worker VibeComfy adapter and early route/selector abstraction.

## Required Deliverables

- `template_routing.py`.
- Executor/adapter seam.
- Backend-neutral resolved-task object.
- Local `REIGH_BACKEND` and static/local selector map.
- Route support states.
- Direct smokes for `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512`.
- One LTX-only or template-independent child smoke.
- Minimal backend/template/profile/error telemetry.
- Include `wan_2_2_t2i` only if its single-frame patch lands here.

## Exit Criteria

Feature-flagged Comfy path works for included direct routes without entering `_convert_to_wgp_task`; child smoke avoids Wan VACE cocktail; unsupported Comfy routes fail closed; local route derivation is test-covered.

User notes and answers:
- Use the recovered critique_output.json findings as live critique context: wire travel_segment/individual_travel_segment, resolve VibeComfy CLI mismatch for resolution/output, specify Python 3.11 VibeComfy CLI interpreter boundary, and run worker tests from reigh-worker/.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 2 Adapter Seam and Local Selector Skeleton

## Overview
`reigh-worker/` already has a single WGP boundary for direct generation tasks: `TaskRegistry._handle_direct_queue_task()` builds a `GenerationTask` through `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_conversion.py`, submits it to `HeadlessTaskQueue`, and the worker later enters `HeadlessTaskQueue._convert_to_wgp_task()` via `source/task_handlers/queue/task_processor.py`. There is also an older generic `source/models/comfy/comfy_handler.py`, but it expects a raw workflow payload and is not the worker-side VibeComfy route selector this sprint needs.

The simplest shape is to add a backend-neutral resolved-task layer before WGP conversion, then let a local selector choose either the existing WGP executor or a new VibeComfy adapter. Supabase queue task types and params stay unchanged; unsupported Comfy routes fail closed by selector state instead of falling through to WGP silently when `REIGH_BACKEND` asks for Comfy.

## Phase 1: Routing Foundation

### Step 1: Add `template_routing.py` (`source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Define route support states, for example `WGP_ONLY`, `COMFY_SUPPORTED`, and `COMFY_UNSUPPORTED`.
2. Add a backend-neutral `ResolvedTask` dataclass with the fields the executors need: `task_id`, `task_type`, `route_key`, `backend`, `support_state`, `template_id`, `model`, `prompt`, `params`, `memory_profile`, and optional `failure_reason`.
3. Implement local route derivation from existing task payloads. Start from the same semantics as `scripts/dual_run_compare/route_keys.py`, but keep production code in `source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py` rather than importing test/comparison script code.
4. Add a static local selector map:
   - `z_image_turbo` -> Comfy template `image/z_image`.
   - `qwen_image_2512` -> Comfy template `image/qwen_image_2512`.
   - one LTX child route, preferably `individual_travel_segment` or `travel_segment` when model family resolves to `ltx2`, mapped to an LTX ready template such as `video/ltx2_3_t2v` only for the smoke-friendly shape.
   - `wan_2_2_t2i` remains WGP-only unless the single-frame patch is part of this implementation.
5. Parse `REIGH_BACKEND` locally with conservative values: unset/`wgp` keeps current behavior; `comfy` selects Comfy only for `COMFY_SUPPORTED`; unsupported routes return a closed failure; optional `auto` can behave like WGP for this sprint unless explicitly requested otherwise.

### Step 2: Add route derivation tests (`tests/test_template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Prove direct route keys for `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` resolve to Comfy-supported template IDs.
2. Prove unsupported Comfy routes fail closed with an explicit support state and reason.
3. Prove local child route derivation for the LTX/template-independent child smoke does not choose a Wan VACE route.
4. Prove `wan_2_2_t2i` is WGP-only unless implemented in the same patch.

## Phase 2: Executor Seam

### Step 3: Split direct queue preparation from WGP execution (`source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep `TaskRegistry.dispatch()` external behavior unchanged.
2. In `_handle_direct_queue_task()`, build a `ResolvedTask` before building or submitting a WGP `GenerationTask`.
3. If the resolved backend is WGP, call the existing `db_task_to_generation_task()` and queue submission path unchanged, including the existing `wan_2_2_t2i` single-frame override.
4. If the resolved backend is Comfy, bypass `HeadlessTaskQueue.submit_task()` and do not enter `_convert_to_wgp_task()`.
5. If the resolved backend is Comfy but support state is not supported, return `(False, <explicit unsupported route message>)` without running WGP.

### Step 4: Add the VibeComfy adapter (`source/models/comfy/vibecomfy_adapter.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Implement a small adapter function, for example `handle_vibecomfy_resolved_task(resolved, main_output_dir_base) -> tuple[bool, str | None]`.
2. Build runtime input from the resolved task: template ID, prompt, seed, steps, resolution, output directory, and `build_memory_profile_cli_args()` from `source/runtime/vibecomfy_profile.py`.
3. Prefer a subprocess CLI boundary for Sprint 2 unless the repo already has a stable vendored Python API available on worker images. This keeps the worker from importing VibeComfy at module import time and preserves the existing profile boundary.
4. Capture adapter errors into normal task failure strings. Do not add retry/fallback to WGP for Comfy-selected unsupported or failed routes in this sprint.
5. Keep the existing raw-workflow `handle_comfy_task()` intact for `task_type == "comfy"`; the new adapter is for selected production routes.

### Step 5: Add executor/adapter tests (`tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter_routing.py` or extend `tests/test_additional_coverage_modules.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Monkeypatch the adapter subprocess call and assert `REIGH_BACKEND=comfy` routes `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` to the adapter.
2. Assert the WGP queue path is not called for those Comfy-supported routes.
3. Assert `REIGH_BACKEND=wgp` or unset preserves the existing direct queue behavior.
4. Assert a Comfy-unsupported route returns a closed failure and does not submit to WGP.

## Phase 3: Telemetry and Smokes

### Step 6: Add minimal telemetry (`source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`, `source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, `source/models/comfy/vibecomfy_adapter.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Log a compact routing card before execution with `task_id`, `task_type`, `route_key`, `backend`, `template_id`, `support_state`, and `memory_profile`.
2. On adapter failure, include backend/template/profile/error in the returned failure message and logs.
3. Avoid changing Supabase task schema or queue contracts.

### Step 7: Add direct and child smoke coverage (`tests/` and optional script fixture reuse)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add direct smoke tests for `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` using monkeypatched adapter execution so CI does not need GPU/Comfy.
2. Add one LTX-only or template-independent child smoke that exercises route derivation and adapter selection without invoking the Wan VACE cocktail path.
3. If `wan_2_2_t2i` single-frame Comfy patch is not included, add an explicit test that it remains WGP-only.

## Execution Order
1. Land `template_routing.py` and route tests first; this is cheap and proves the selector decisions before touching execution.
2. Wire `_handle_direct_queue_task()` through `ResolvedTask` while preserving WGP as the default path.
3. Add the VibeComfy adapter behind `REIGH_BACKEND=comfy`.
4. Add telemetry and smoke tests after the seam is stable.
5. Only promote `wan_2_2_t2i` to Comfy-supported if its single-frame patch is implemented and tested in the same change.

## Validation Order
1. Run focused unit tests first: `uv run pytest tests/test_template_routing.py tests/runtime/test_vibecomfy_profile.py`.
2. Run routing/adapter smoke tests: `uv run pytest tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter_routing.py tests/test_task_type_catalog.py`.
3. Run existing boundary tests that guard registry behavior: `uv run pytest tests/test_boundary_behavioral_smoke.py tests/test_task_dispatch_manifest_contracts.py tests/test_join_orchestrator_and_registry.py`.
4. Finish with the relevant broader worker suite if runtime allows: `uv run pytest tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py tests/test_lora_flow.py`.

## Notes on Scope
This sprint should not change Supabase task payloads, claim/selector production DB behavior, or orchestrator ownership. The local selector is deliberately static and feature-flagged. Full production selector/claim work waits for Sprint 6.

        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",

      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",

      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",

      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
# Sprint 2: Adapter Seam and Local Selector Skeleton

## Overall Context

This sprint creates the first worker-side VibeComfy seam. It depends on Sprint 1 profile behavior and deliberately proves local route selection before production selector/claim work arrives in Sprint 6.

## Shared Operating Rules

- Keep `reigh-worker/` external Supabase queue contracts unchanged.
- Backend-neutral resolved tasks must exist before WGP-specific queue conversion.
- Unsupported Comfy routes fail closed and remain WGP-only.
- Do not require Wan VACE cocktail or full orchestrated parity here.

## Sprint Goal

Add the worker VibeComfy adapter and early route/selector abstraction.

## Required Deliverables

- `template_routing.py`.
- Executor/adapter seam.
- Backend-neutral resolved-task object.
- Local `REIGH_BACKEND` and static/local selector map.
- Route support states.
- Direct smokes for `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512`.
- One LTX-only or template-independent child smoke.
- Minimal backend/template/profile/error telemetry.
- Include `wan_2_2_t2i` only if its single-frame patch lands here.

## Exit Criteria

Feature-flagged Comfy path works for included direct routes without entering `_convert_to_wgp_task`; child smoke avoids Wan VACE cocktail; unsupported Comfy routes fail closed; local route derivation is test-covered.

User notes and answers:
- Use the recovered critique_output.json findings as live critique context: wire travel_segment/individual_travel_segment, resolve VibeComfy CLI mismatch for resolution/output, specify Python 3.11 VibeComfy CLI interpreter boundary, and run worker tests from reigh-worker/.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 2 Adapter Seam and Local Selector Skeleton

## Overview
The critique does not show that the plan targeted the wrong root cause; it shows the seam was too narrow. The root cause is still the same: backend selection must happen before any WGP-specific `GenerationTask` reaches `HeadlessTaskQueue.submit_task()`, because `submit_task()` pre-validates through `_convert_to_wgp_task()`. The revised approach keeps the backend-neutral resolved-task layer, but moves the execution seam into every queue-backed generation path that can submit WGP work: direct queue dispatch in `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py` and travel child queue submission in `source/task_handlers/travel/segments/segment_queue.py`.

`reigh-worker/` remains the implementation and validation root. External Supabase task contracts stay unchanged. `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy` is the canonical feature flag value; unset or `wgp` preserves current WGP behavior. Explicit VibeComfy selection is strict fail-closed for unsupported routes. The VibeComfy adapter must run across a Python 3.11 subprocess boundary because `reigh-worker` is Python 3.10-constrained while `vibecomfy` requires Python 3.11.

## Phase 1: Routing Foundation

### Step 1: Add backend-neutral routing (`source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Define route support states: `WGP_ONLY`, `VIBECOMFY_SUPPORTED`, and `VIBECOMFY_UNSUPPORTED`.
2. Add a backend-neutral `ResolvedTask` dataclass with executor-facing fields: `task_id`, `task_type`, `route_key`, `backend`, `support_state`, `template_id`, `model`, `prompt`, `params`, `memory_profile`, and optional `failure_reason`.
3. Add `resolve_task_backend(...)` that accepts raw task params plus task context and returns a `ResolvedTask` before any WGP conversion.
4. Implement production-local route derivation in this module, using the same route-key semantics as `scripts/dual_run_compare/route_keys.py` but not importing script/test utilities into worker runtime code.
5. Add the static Sprint 2 selector map:
   - `z_image_turbo` -> `image/z_image`, VibeComfy-supported.
   - `qwen_image_2512` -> `image/qwen_image_2512`, VibeComfy-supported.
   - LTX child smoke route for `travel_segment` and `individual_travel_segment` when model family resolves to `ltx2`, mapped only to the selected smoke-safe LTX template or marked template-independent if the adapter test only proves routing.
   - `wan_2_2_t2i` -> WGP-only unless the single-frame VibeComfy patch is implemented and tested in this same change.
6. Parse `REIGH_BACKEND` with canonical values: unset/`wgp` means WGP; `vibecomfy` means strict VibeComfy selection. Do not introduce fallback-to-WGP behavior under explicit `vibecomfy`.

### Step 2: Cover route derivation (`tests/test_template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Prove `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` resolve to VibeComfy-supported template IDs under `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy`.
2. Prove unsupported routes fail closed with `VIBECOMFY_UNSUPPORTED` and an explicit reason under `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy`.
3. Prove LTX child route derivation for both `travel_segment` and `individual_travel_segment` avoids Wan VACE/cocktail route keys.
4. Prove `wan_2_2_t2i` remains WGP-only unless the single-frame patch is explicitly added.
5. Prove unset and `REIGH_BACKEND=wgp` preserve WGP routing.

## Phase 2: Shared Executor Seam

### Step 3: Add a shared resolved-task executor (`source/task_handlers/tasks/task_execution.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a small executor helper that receives `ResolvedTask`, context, and a WGP generation-task builder callback.
2. For `backend == wgp`, call the provided builder and submit to `HeadlessTaskQueue` exactly as today.
3. For `backend == vibecomfy`, call the VibeComfy adapter directly and bypass `HeadlessTaskQueue.submit_task()` so `_convert_to_wgp_task()` is not reached.
4. For unsupported VibeComfy routes, return `(False, <explicit unsupported route message>)` without WGP fallback.
5. Keep this helper independent of Supabase schema and claim logic so Sprint 6 can replace only the selector source, not every call site.

### Step 4: Wire direct queue dispatch (`source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. In `_handle_direct_queue_task()`, resolve backend before calling `db_task_to_generation_task()`.
2. Preserve current WGP behavior for unset/`wgp`, including `wan_2_2_t2i` `video_length=1`, colour matching, and mask-active params.
3. Under `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy`, route `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` through the shared executor and adapter.
4. Add tests that monkeypatch the adapter and queue to assert VibeComfy-selected direct routes never submit to the WGP queue.

### Step 5: Wire travel child submission (`source/task_handlers/travel/segments/segment_queue.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Locate the `GenerationTask` construction and `task_queue.submit_task()` path used by `handle_travel_segment_via_queue`.
2. Resolve backend for both `travel_segment` and `individual_travel_segment` child tasks before calling `submit_task()`.
3. For WGP-selected routes, preserve current travel behavior and queue waiting semantics.
4. For VibeComfy-selected LTX/template-independent child smoke routes, call the shared executor/adapter path and bypass `submit_task()`.
5. Under explicit `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy`, fail closed for unsupported travel child routes so Wan VACE/cocktail routes do not run as accidental WGP work.

## Phase 3: VibeComfy Adapter Boundary

### Step 6: Add the Python 3.11 subprocess adapter (`source/models/comfy/vibecomfy_adapter.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Implement `handle_vibecomfy_resolved_task(resolved, main_output_dir_base) -> tuple[bool, str | None]`.
2. Use an explicit interpreter boundary. Read `VIBECOMFY_PYTHON` first, then fall back to `python3.11`; never import VibeComfy into worker module import paths.
3. Run the command from the VibeComfy project/install context, configurable with `VIBECOMFY_CWD` or package installation. Tests should monkeypatch subprocess execution rather than requiring local VibeComfy installation.
4. Match the current `vibecomfy run` CLI surface. Do not pass unsupported `--resolution` or `--output-directory` arguments.
5. Pass only supported CLI options for Sprint 2, such as ready template id, prompt, seed, steps, and `build_memory_profile_cli_args()` output where supported.
6. For resolution or other template-specific values not exposed by the current CLI, generate a temporary VibeComfy scratchpad/input file only if the current VibeComfy CLI supports running that file; otherwise leave those fields out of the adapter command and document them as not wired for Sprint 2.
7. Since the current CLI has no output-directory option, run in an isolated per-task working directory or inspect the VibeComfy result/stdout/default output location to discover the produced artifact. Return the resolved artifact path to the existing worker completion flow.
8. Capture stdout/stderr/exit code and return a normal task failure string with backend, template, profile, and error summary.
9. Keep `source/models/comfy/comfy_handler.py` unchanged for raw `task_type == "comfy"` workflow payloads.

### Step 7: Add adapter command tests (`tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Assert the adapter uses `VIBECOMFY_PYTHON` or `python3.11`, not the worker interpreter by default.
2. Assert the subprocess command does not include unsupported `--resolution` or `--output-directory` flags.
3. Assert memory profile CLI args from `source/runtime/vibecomfy_profile.py` are included when resolved.
4. Assert subprocess failures include backend/template/profile/error telemetry in the returned message.
5. Assert output discovery returns a produced artifact path from the isolated run directory or parsed command result.

## Phase 4: Telemetry and Smokes

### Step 8: Add minimal routing telemetry (`source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_execution.py`, `source/models/comfy/vibecomfy_adapter.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Log one compact routing card before execution with `task_id`, `task_type`, `route_key`, `backend`, `template_id`, `support_state`, and `memory_profile`.
2. Log adapter completion/failure with backend, template, profile, exit code, and bounded stderr summary.
3. Add automated tests that monkeypatch the logger and assert the key telemetry fields are emitted for successful route selection and failure.
4. Do not add Supabase columns or mutate queue payload contracts.

### Step 9: Add direct and child smoke tests (`tests/test_vibecomfy_backend_selection.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add direct smoke tests for `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` with monkeypatched adapter execution.
2. Add child smoke coverage for both the travel queue seam and route derivation, using an LTX-only or template-independent child payload that does not exercise Wan VACE/cocktail generation.
3. Assert VibeComfy-selected direct and child smokes bypass `HeadlessTaskQueue.submit_task()` and therefore cannot enter `_convert_to_wgp_task()`.
4. Assert unsupported VibeComfy direct and child routes fail closed.

## Execution Order
1. Add `template_routing.py` and route tests first.
2. Add the shared executor helper and wire direct queue dispatch while preserving WGP default behavior.
3. Wire `travel_segment` and `individual_travel_segment` queue submission through the same resolved-task executor.
4. Add the Python 3.11 VibeComfy subprocess adapter with command-surface tests.
5. Add telemetry and end-to-end mocked smoke tests.
6. Only promote `wan_2_2_t2i` to VibeComfy-supported if its single-frame patch is implemented and covered in the same branch.

## Validation Order
1. From `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker`, run focused routing tests: `uv run pytest tests/test_template_routing.py tests/runtime/test_vibecomfy_profile.py`.
2. From `reigh-worker/`, run adapter command tests: `uv run pytest tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`.
3. From `reigh-worker/`, run backend-selection smokes: `uv run pytest tests/test_vibecomfy_backend_selection.py tests/test_task_type_catalog.py`.
4. From `reigh-worker/`, run registry and travel boundary tests: `uv run pytest tests/test_boundary_behavioral_smoke.py tests/test_task_dispatch_manifest_contracts.py tests/test_join_orchestrator_and_registry.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py`.
5. If a real Python 3.11 VibeComfy runtime and GPU/Comfy dependencies are available, run manual live smokes for `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512`; these remain info-level, not automated must gates.

## Notes on Scope
This sprint does not implement production selector/claim work, Supabase schema changes, full orchestrated parity, or Wan VACE cocktail parity. The local selector is static, worker-local, and feature-flagged. The adapter is intentionally a subprocess seam so the incompatible Python versions do not leak into worker imports.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-153",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: worker integration: the migration doc describes the per-call profile override gap as involving reigh-worker adapter handling of override_profile before constructing vibecomfy sessionconfig, but the plan limits sprint 1 per-run work to the vibecomfy cli/runtime. that may be acceptable for a vibecomfy-library mvp, but it does not fully exercise the production-facing per-task override semantics called out in the overall context.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-154",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    }

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "concern": "is the scope and scale of the change appropriate?: phase 2 is still under-specified for execution. step 4 says pairregionslayer should either pass `pairdatabyindex.get(pairindex)` directly or 'just pass the index + frame-only data', which are materially different designs. if phase 2 is kept in the plan, it needs a single concrete direction.",

  "is-there-convincing-verification-for-the-change": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: the plan still lacks an explicit automated regression test for the reported bug. step 2 is manual ('check that `usevideoregeneratemode` now gets the fresh url'), but there is no concrete test that opens a segment slot, changes the primary variant data, and asserts that the regenerate path sees the fresh `url`, `generationid`, and `primaryvariantid`.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: the current `usesegmentslotmode` test remains only a smoke test in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/__tests__/usesegmentslotmode.test.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/__tests__/usesegmentslotmode.test.ts), and the plan does not add behavior coverage for the new sync effect.",

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
